## China Consumer

Cheap but unloved – stay selective while waiting for the earnings floor

## China Consumer

Jessie Xu AC

Phone: +852 2800-8590

E-mail: jessie.j.xu@JPM.com

## Key thesis

China Consumer Top Picks: (1) Nongfu (OW): strong brand momentum and margin buffer; (2) Anta (OW): well-executed multi-brand portfolio and overseas upside; (3) Guming (OW): continued runway for network growth and improving brand equity.

Earnings revisions YTD: 2026 consensus EPS is down 9% for Staples and 7% for Discretionary. 80% of our coverage is down YTD, led by Baijiu, beauty, etc. Winners on revisions are fewer but real: Guming, Laopu, Li Ning, and Nongfu. “Full pricing” of cost pressure may not show up until late 2026.  
What if the Iran conflict ended now? Packaging and industrial inputs (e.g. PE/PET) are up 15-40% vs the 2025 average. JPM China Economics team forecasts PPI at 3.5%/3.9%/3.5% in 2-4Q26 and CPI at 1.3%/1.4%/1.1%, widening the CPI-PPI gap to -2.9pp. In this tape, Baijiu and beer look more insulated (less cut-throat competition), while beverages are more exposed. Pricing power matters: Moutai, major brewers, and Haitian should be better positioned to pass costs through.  
Alpha comes from operator quality, not beta. Demand may stay headline-noisy, but the real story is the split underneath. Operator quality – not broad category beta – should be the primary source of alpha. Based on our scoring across fundamentals (demand/supply/pricing/margins), returns (dividends), and valuation, sportswear, pop toys, and white appliances stand out over the next 3-5 years. In our coverage, we like Anta, Pop Mart, and Midea.  
Catalysts to watch and strategy: Cost inflation hitting from 3Q26 is a headwind for beverages. Mid-Autumn Festival and October Golden Week are key read-throughs for baijiu and gold/jewelry.

Positioning: add FMD after June (Guming, Luckin, Chagee); avoid beverages (especially crowded dividend trades) in 3Q26. Comps get friendlier over time: Moutai (N) from 3Q26, FMD and Pop Mart (N) from 4Q26, and beverages from 2Q27.

## Sector View Updates

Specialty retail – more than just a craze. The surging demand for pop toys is not a passing fad but represents long-term structural growth, in our view. Our TAM analysis, using both population and GDP benchmarks, suggests a potential store count of 1,000-3,000 for Pop Mart, implying 76% to 4x upside from the current base (630 stores at end-2025), and a 5-year revenue CAGR of 12%-40%.  
- Bubble & Brew – who is still running? Divergence is emerging in expansion: Luckin and Guming to grow store counts by approx. 31% and 51% through 2027, while Mixue (mainly Lucky Cup) should decelerate as network saturation sets in. Boundaries between coffee, tea and bakery are blurring. Price competition has eased in 2026 YTD, with players pivoting from aggressive discounting toward higher-ASP product launches.  
- RTD beverage – demand thriving while competition intensifying. Sports drink is the fastest-growing category within China's soft drinks market, 14.7% CAGR in 2019-25 and 11% through 2030E. Competition is intensifying, with Danone (OW, covered by Celine Pannuti), Eastroc, Nongfu Spring, and Chi Forest (not covered) competing head-to-head (market shares of 13-19% each). Cost pressure from PET price sensitivity remains a key theme into 2H26, with Nongfu positioned as the most resilient player (30% NPM in 2025).  
Baijiu – the dawn is not far off; Moutai DTC under the spotlight. The baijiu sector's prosperity is shaped by three overlapping cycles – macro (including property), supply-demand (pricing), and policy – and the industry is approaching the late stages of its downturn and the early innings of recovery. Property prices in tier-1 cities are showing tentative signs of stabilization. Moutai's DTC reform successfully expands its customer base to mass-market while wholesale prices have stabilized above Rmb1,600.  
- Beer – embracing new channels. China's beer market has entered volume maturity, with the growth algorithm decisively shifting to premiumization and a structural channel migration from on-trade to off-trade. CR Beer is best positioned with 70% off-trade mix. An early and hot summer could act as catalysts.

# RTD Beverages – Sports drinks shine, but cost pressure bites

## Sports drinks under the spotlight; competition intensifying

Sports drinks are projected to grow at a 2025–30E CAGR of 10.9%, more than double the overall soft drinks market growth of 4.0%. Revenue momentum has visibly inflected since 2023, with YoY growth accelerating to approximately 15%, signaling that the category has entered a new phase of expansion.

## RTD Beverages – Sports drinks shine, but cost pressure bites

## Sports drinks under the spotlight; competition intensifying

This attractive growth profile has drawn aggressive participation from major players.

- Eastroc's "Water Boost" has been the standout performer, scaling its sports beverage revenue from near zero in 2023 to an estimated Rmb 8bn by 2028E, with market share rising rapidly towards $20\%$ .  
Nongfu Spring launched new Electrolyte Water product in 2026, aiming to capture incremental share alongside its existing Scream and Victory Vitamin Water lines, bringing its combined portfolio share to approximately 18% by 2026E.

The market was once dominated by Mizone (Danone), but is now rapidly fragmenting as domestic challengers deploy new products, refreshed packaging, and gamified promotions (e.g., QR-code lotteries and coupon mechanics) to drive trial and repeat purchases.

## RTD Beverages – Sports drinks shine, but cost pressure bites

## Cost pressure a major theme into 2H26; Nongfu most resilient

Our sensitivity test shows that for every $10\%$ PET price increase, Nongfu / Eastroc / CR Beverage's gross profit will be impacted by $1\% / 3\% / 4\%$ , net earnings will be cut by $3\% / 8\% / 21\%$ and net margin will decline by 0.8pp / 1.5pp / 1.9pp on an annualized basis.  
Currently, the PET is priced c.40% higher vs 2025 average levels. Most RTD beverage companies have locked in PET supply and prices through 2Q26, and hence will need to renew contracts relatively soon.

EPS sensitivity to PET price hike

## Bubble & Brew – After the tide recedes, who is still running?

## After the tide retreats

After fast growth in 2025, we observe a clear divergence in store expansion trajectories among China's leading beverage chains heading into 2026-2027: Luckin and Guming to remain in aggressive growth mode, with store counts up $31\%$ and $51\%$ in 2025-27E. Chagee, with the highest AOV at Rmb18.3, is also set to grow its footprint by $15\%$ over the same period, albeit from a smaller base.  
Lucky Cup and Mixue will enter a phase of meaningful deceleration. Lucky Cup's net store count is expected to remain essentially flat, while Mixue's expansion moderates to +17%, from 7k-8k annual net openings in 2023-2024 to well below 5k by 2026-27E. This slowdown likely reflects network saturation at the lower price tier (AOVs of Rmb 8.1 and Rmb 7.3 for Lucky Cup and Mixue) after years of hyper-expansion.

We expect Luckin and Guming to further grow store count by c.30-50% in the next two years

## Bubble & Brew – After the tide recedes, who is still running?

## Blurring boundaries: How category convergence is reshaping the landscape

## Argument 1:

## Coffee penetration remains in early innings, offering a long runway for tea brands crossing over

We forecast a 14% CAGR of China’s total coffee consumption through 2040, reaching 145bn cups. Implied per capita consumption of 108 cups would represent only 26% of South Korea's current level of 416 cups (2024). The vast whitespace explains why tea-centric brands such as Guming and Mixue are actively adding coffee to their menus – they can leverage existing store networks to capture incremental demand in an under-penetrated category without significant capital outlay.

We forecast China's total coffee consumption to grow at a $14\%$ CAGR from 2026 to 2040 ...  
![](images/b44957b2d1e604a7b9898612172105c49fb74fb5edccf1319844ca07821dce26.jpg)

| Year | Value (bn cups) |
| :--- | :--- |
| 2019 | 8 |
| 2026E | 24 |
| 2040E | 145 |
CAGR 17% from 2019 to 2026E, CAGR 14% from 2026E to 2040E
</details>

Source: Customs data, Ministry of Agriculture of China, JPM.

... with per capita consumption reaching 108 cups by 2040, only equivalent to $26\%$ of South Korea's level in 2024

## Beer – Right side of the channel migration

## YTD performance: Diverging trajectories among the big three

The channel migration has produced differentiated performance among the major brewers YTD. All three brewers are converging on a common playbook: optimizing product portfolios, extending strength in their respective advantaged segments, and building capabilities for the off-trade channel.

Bud China, historically over-indexed to nightlife and high-end dining channels, has been undergoing a strategic pivot since 2025 - refocusing on the Rmb 6–8 price band with core SKUs including Classic Budweiser, Hoegaarden, and Corona, while accelerating the recovery of Harbin Beer. This transition has come at a cost: organic sales declined 11% in 2025 and remained negative at around -4% in 1Q26.  
CR Beer continues to deliver steady, positive momentum, leveraging its structural advantage in off-trade channels (70% off-trade mix - the highest among major players). The company is driving growth through its core brands - Brave the World, Pure Draft, Marrs Green - and the premium Heineken series. Sales grew \~1% in 1Q26 and accelerated to \~2% from April onward, with volume growth of 2–3%, reflecting continued progress on premiumization upgrades.  
Tsingtao, with a higher on-trade skew (60% on-trade / 40% off-trade), is navigating the channel shift by centering its strategy on Draft Beer as the hero SKU, targeting the Rmb 6–8 and Rmb 8+ craft segments, and expanding 1-liter formats tailored to family and home-banquet occasions. April sales grew 2.4% with volume up 3–4%, indicating that its product structure optimization and format innovation are gaining traction.

Cost resilience: Beer margins better insulated than RTD beverages; YTD beer cost index only up 3.9%

## Baijiu – Approaching the trough

## Where are we in the cycle?

The baijiu sector's prosperity is shaped by the convergence of three overlapping cycles:

1) Macro cycle; 2) Supply-demand and pricing cycle; 3) Policy cycle.

## Macro cycle: Baijiu growth decoupling from a slowing economy

Industry revenue grew at a rapid pace in 2005-2012 - buoyed by double-digit GDP growth and a fixed asset investment boom. As China's structural growth rate has decelerated, baijiu industry revenue growth has also downshifted, turning negative in 2025 at around -18% yoy.  
With fixed asset investment growth now hovering in the low single digits and GDP growth stabilizing around 4–5%, the macro tailwinds that once propelled the industry have largely dissipated. We expect industry revenue to begin stabilizing in 2026E, though the recovery trajectory will be more muted than in prior cycles given the structurally lower growth backdrop.

## Baijiu – Approaching the trough

## Where are we in the cycle?

## Household balance sheets recovering: Property prices showing early signs of stabilization

JPM forecasts that tier-1 city home prices will see a soft form of stabilization in 2026 (average -1% to 0% YoY). Beyond tier-1 cities, the broader national picture remains more cautious but is approaching a floor.  
For baijiu, household balance sheet repair should begin to alleviate the wealth destruction effect that has weighed on high-end consumption and banquet spending over the past two years.

## Supply-demand & policy cycle: Mapping 2024-to-date downcycle against 2012-16

In the prior cycle, the trigger was a sudden policy shock, sending industry revenue into decline for seven consecutive quarters before an inflection in 2H15. The current cycle, began with a more gradual deterioration, and the “alcohol ban (禁酒令)” arrived in 2Q25 added further downward pressure.  
If the 2012–2016 playbook is any guide, the industry may face several more quarters of contraction before bottoming. However, the current downcycle entered its demand weakness phase earlier, suggesting the industry may already be further along in its adjustment process than headline revenue declines imply.

# Baijiu – Approaching the trough

Where are we in the cycle?

## Our assessment: Approaching the trough, but recovery will be gradual

Synthesizing across all three cycles, we believe the baijiu sector is in the early innings of recovery.

- On the macro front, GDP growth has stabilized and property prices are showing tentative signs of bottoming – laying the groundwork for a gradual recovery in household wealth confidence.  
On the supply-demand and policy front, the industry has already absorbed five quarters of revenue decline and the incremental policy shock of the alcohol ban; if the 2012–2016 analog holds, the trough may arrive within the next two to three quarters.

However, unlike the V-shaped recovery of 2016–2017, we expect the rebound to be more L-shaped - constrained by structurally softer GDP growth, a still-fragile property market outside tier-1 cities, and a more cautious consumer mindset. The setup favors patient positioning in quality names with pricing power and DTC optionality, rather than aggressive cyclical bets on a sharp snapback.

## Baijiu – Approaching the trough

DTC Reform: the next big thing, led by Moutai

## A paradigm shift in distribution: From wholesale dependence to direct-to-consumer

The market has responded positively to each of Moutai's DTC reform milestones.  
- Investors are increasingly pricing in the structural benefits of DTC – namely, greater pricing transparency, reduced channel speculation, and a more direct relationship with end consumers.

\* Net payables  
\* Deducted net sales  
Revenue was recognized, but the goods are still sitting in the company's warehouse (valued at production cost)  
Payment was received but goods were not shipped, so revenue cannot be recognized (valued at ex-factory price)  
Revenue reversed for the portion where goods have not been shipped

## Explanation of Equity Research Ratings, Designations and Analyst(s) Coverage Universe:

JPM uses the following rating system: Overweight (over the duration of the price target indicated in this report, we expect this stock will outperform the average total return of the stocks in the Research Analyst's, or the Research Analyst's team's, coverage universe); Neutral (over the duration of the price target indicated in this report, we expect this stock will perform in line with the average total return of the stocks in the Research Analyst's, or the Research Analyst's team's, coverage universe); and Underweight (over the duration of the price target indicated in this report, we expect this stock will underperform the average total return of the stocks in the Research Analyst's, or the Research Analyst's team's, coverage universe. NR is Not Rated. In this case, JPM has removed the rating and, if applicable, the price target, for this stock because of either a lack of a sufficient fundamental basis or for legal, regulatory or policy reasons. The previous rating and, if applicable, the price target, no longer should be relied upon. An NR designation is not a recommendation or a rating. Some stocks under coverage have a rating but no price target; in these cases, we expect the stock will outperform/perform in line/underperform the average total return of the stocks in the Research Analyst's, or the Research Analyst's team's, coverage universe of the relevant duration of the region. In our Asia (ex-Australia and ex-India) and U.K. small- and mid-cap Equity Research, each stock's expected total return is compared to the expected total return of a benchmark country market index, not to those Research Analysts' coverage universe. If it does not appear in the Important Disclosures section of this report, the certifying Research Analyst's coverage universe can be found on JPM's Research website, https://www.JPMmarkets.com.
